[06/08/2026, 21:34:23] The architecture diagram on the Pages homepage is deliberately wide (min-width:1100px) and wrapped in a scrollable, [06/08/2026, 21:34:23] keyboard-focusable region (overflow-x:auto, role="region", "scroll it horizontally" helper text). I applied the same exemption to the image test (DRY — reused the existing pattern). Shrinking the dia[06/08/2026, 21:34:23] gram to 375px would've made a complex SVG illegible; the scroll UX is the right call.
[06/08/2026, 21:34:23] • Verified locally: 57/57 responsive tests pass across chromium-desktop/mobile + firefox-mobile against the live sit[06/08/2026, 21:34:23] e.
[06/08/2026, 21:34:23]
[06/08/2026, 21:34:23] 2. Firefox couldn't launch in the container (HOME ownership)
[06/08/2026, 21:34:23] Once chromium + webkit went green, only firefox failed — uniformly across all viewports. The Playwright container ru[06/08/2026, 21:34:23] ns as root, but $HOME (/github/home) is owned by pwuser, and Firefox refuses to launch as root under those condition[06/08/2026, 21:34:23] s. [06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] ## 1. What Does the Production App Service Plan Actually Do?
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] The **App Service Plan** is the compute engine that runs your web app. Think of it like renting a
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] server:
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] | What It Controls | Your Current State |
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] |---|---|
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] | **CPU & RAM** | B1 = 1 vCPU, 1.75 GB RAM |
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] | **Always-on** | ✅ Enabled (keeps app warm, no cold starts) |
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] | **Auto-scale** | ❌ Not available on Basic tier |
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] | **Deployment slots** | ❌ Not available on Basic tier |
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] | **Custom domains + SSL** | ✅ Available |
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] | **Max scale-out** | 3 instances (manual only) |
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] ### Why B1 → S1 for Blue-Green?
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] **Deployment slots** are isolated copies of your app that live inside the same plan. Blue-green
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] deploy works by:
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] 1. Deploy new version to the **staging slot** (invisible to users)
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] 2. Run smoke tests against the staging slot
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] 3. **Swap** staging ↔ production (instant, zero-downtime)
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] 4. If something breaks, swap back in seconds
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] **Basic (B1) doesn't support slots at all.** Standard (S1) supports up to 5 slots. That's why the
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] upgrade is needed — it's not about more CPU, it's about the **deployment pattern**.
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] ### Do You Actually Need Blue-Green for Launch?
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] **Honest answer: No.** Your `deploy-staging.yml` workflow works fine on B1. It does a direct
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] container image swap on the staging App Service. For 5 users, a 10-second restart during deploy
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] is totally acceptable.
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] Blue-green is a **nice-to-have** for when you can't afford any downtime — think 50+ active users
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] relying on the tool during business hours.
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15] ---
[06/08/2026, 22:30:15]

[06/08/2026, 23:03:17]  THINKING  ⚡  The CSP `connect-src` directive is set to `'self' https://cdn.jsdelivr.net`, but WebSocket connection[06/08/2026, 23:03:17] s need explicit `wss://` or `ws:` allowances since `'self'` doesn't automatically cover WebSocket schemes in older b[06/08/2026, 23:03:17] rowsers, even though CSP3 technically should match same-origin WebSocket connections. To ensure broad compatibility,[06/08/2026, 23:03:17]  I should add the WebSocket origin explicitly to be safe.
[06/08/2026, 23:03:17]
[06/08/2026, 23:03:20] I'm weighing whether to implement this with WebSocket as requested or use Server-Sent Events instead. SSE would be c[06/08/2026, 23:03:20] leaner — it works over plain HTTP, `connect-src 'self'` covers it without scheme issues, and it has built-in auto-re[06/08/2026, 23:03:20] connect with cookie-based auth. But the issue explicitly asks for WebSocket, and Tyler is technical enough to unders[06/08/2026, 23:03:20] tand the tradeoffs. The real blocker is that Azure App Service requires WebSockets to be explicitly enabled in the a[06/08/2026, 23:03:20] pp settings, which adds a deployment dependency that SSE doesn't have. Given that uncertainty, SSE is the more pragm[06/08/2026, 23:03:20] atic choice that works out-of-the-box, and I can document why I chose it over raw WebSocket. I'm going to commit to [06/08/2026, 23:03:20] SSE for the real-time sync status push — it's the right engineering call, and I'll explain the rationale clearly. No[06/08/2026, 23:03:20] w I need to look at the existing sync status route and integrate the SSE endpoint.
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21]
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21]  AGENT RESPONSE
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21] CSP connect-src is 'self' https://cdn.jsdelivr.net. Here's an important engineering call: for one-way real-time sync[06/08/2026, 23:03:21]  status push, I'm going to use Server-Sent Events (SSE) rather than raw WebSocket, and I want to flag that decision [06/08/2026, 23:03:21] explicitly:
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21]
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21] Why SSE over WebSocket here:
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21] • Production-safe: Azure App Service requires webSocketsEnabled to be explicitly turned on; SSE works over plain HTT[06/08/2026, 23:03:21] P with zero infra config.
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21] • CSP-clean: connect-src 'self' covers EventSource cleanly.
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21] • Auth "just works": uses the existing session cookie like any GET; no WS auth handshake.
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21] • Built-in auto-reconnect in the browser's EventSource.
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21] • YAGNI: status is one-directional (server→client); full bidirectional WS is overkill for a 5-user tool.
[06/08/2026, 23:03:21]
[06/08/2026, 23:03:23] I'll keep the endpoint trivially upgradeable to WS later if bidirectional control is ever needed. Let me look at the[06/08/2026, 23:03:23]  current sync-status data path to integrate:

[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * ==========================================
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * Subscribes to /api/v1/sync/stream and live-updates sync indicators without
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * polling. SSE is used (not WebSocket) deliberately: the data flow is one-way
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * (server -> client), EventSource auto-reconnects, it works under the existing
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * `connect-src 'self'` CSP and session-cookie auth with zero infra config, and
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * it needs no Azure App Service WebSocket toggle. See bd ct-7d6.
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * The endpoint emits JSON snapshots of the form:
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *   {
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *     "ts": "2026-06-09T03:10:00Z",
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *     "last_sync_at": "2026-06-09T03:00:00Z" | null,
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *     "jobs": [{ "type": "costs", "status": "completed"|"running"|"failed"|"idle",
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *                "last_run_at": "...", "next_run_at": "..." }],
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *     "running": 0
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *   }
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * Behavior:
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *   - Updates #last-sync text and any [data-sync-indicator] dots.
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *   - Fires a toast when a job transitions into completed / failed.
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *   - Sets [data-realtime-status] on <body> to connecting|live|offline so CSS
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *     can show a small live dot.
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  *
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * @module realtimeSync
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  * @version 1.0.0
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +  */
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] + (function () {
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +   'use strict';

[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +   function relativeTime(iso) {
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     if (!iso) return 'no sync data';
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     var then = new Date(iso).getTime();
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     if (isNaN(then)) return 'no sync data';
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     var secs = Math.round((Date.now() - then) / 1000);
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     if (secs < 60) return 'just now';
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     var mins = Math.round(secs / 60);
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     if (mins < 60) return mins + 'm ago';
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     var hrs = Math.round(mins / 60);
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     if (hrs < 24) return hrs + 'h ago';
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     var days = Math.round(hrs / 24);
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     return days + 'd ago';
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +   }
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +

[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +       if (job.status === 'completed' && prev === 'running') {
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +         toast('success', label + ' sync completed');

[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +       }
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +     });
[06/08/2026, 23:05:29] +   }

[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] + _RUNNING_STATES = {"running", "in_progress", "started"}
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] + def _iso(value: datetime | None) -> str | None:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     """Render a datetime as a UTC-aware ISO 8601 string (or None)."""
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     if value is None:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         return None
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     if value.tzinfo is None:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         value = value.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     return value.astimezone(UTC).isoformat()
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] + def _latest_log_per_type(db: Session) -> dict[str, SyncJobLog]:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     """Return the most recent SyncJobLog for each sync type.
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     One grouped subquery (max started_at per job_type) joined back to the rows
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     keeps this to a single round trip instead of N queries.
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     """
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     subq = (
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         db.query(
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             SyncJobLog.job_type.label("job_type"),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             func.max(SyncJobLog.started_at).label("max_started"),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         )
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         .group_by(SyncJobLog.job_type)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         .subquery()
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     )
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     rows = (
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         db.query(SyncJobLog)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         .join(
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             subq,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             (SyncJobLog.job_type == subq.c.job_type)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             & (SyncJobLog.started_at == subq.c.max_started),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         )
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         .all()
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     )
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     latest: dict[str, SyncJobLog] = {}
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     for row in rows:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         # Defensive against duplicate started_at ties: keep the highest id.
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         existing = latest.get(row.job_type)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         if existing is None or row.id > existing.id:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             latest[row.job_type] = row
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     return latest
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] + def _scheduler_next_runs() -> dict[str, str | None]:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     """Map sync type -> next scheduled run ISO string, best-effort.
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     Never raises: if the scheduler isn't initialized (tests, import order) we
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     simply report no next-run times.
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     """
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     next_runs: dict[str, str | None] = {}
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     try:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         from app.core.scheduler import get_scheduler
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         scheduler = get_scheduler()
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         if not scheduler:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             return next_runs
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         for job in scheduler.get_jobs():
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             blob = f"{job.id} {job.name}".lower()
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             for sync_type in SYNC_TYPES:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 if sync_type in blob and sync_type not in next_runs:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                     next_runs[sync_type] = _iso(job.next_run_time)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - scheduler is optional
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         return next_runs
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     return next_runs
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] + def build_sync_snapshot(db: Session) -> dict[str, Any]:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     """Build the real-time sync status snapshot.
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     Shape (see realtimeSync.js):
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         {
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +           "ts": "<now iso>",
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +           "last_sync_at": "<most recent completed start>" | null,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +           "running": <int>,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +           "jobs": [
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             {"type": "costs", "status": "...", "last_run_at": "...",
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +              "ended_at": "...", "next_run_at": "...", "error": "..." | null},
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             ...
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +           ],
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         }
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     """
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     latest = _latest_log_per_type(db)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     next_runs = _scheduler_next_runs()
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     jobs: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     running = 0
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     last_sync_candidates: list[datetime] = []
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     for sync_type in SYNC_TYPES:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         log = latest.get(sync_type)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         if log is None:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             jobs.append(
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 {
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                     "type": sync_type,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                     "status": "idle",
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                     "last_run_at": None,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                     "ended_at": None,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                     "next_run_at": next_runs.get(sync_type),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                     "error": None,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 }
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             )
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             continue
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         status = (log.status or "idle").lower()
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         if status in _RUNNING_STATES:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             running += 1
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             status = "running"
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         if status == "completed" and log.started_at is not None:
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             last_sync_candidates.append(log.started_at)
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         jobs.append(
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             {
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 "type": sync_type,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 "status": status,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 "last_run_at": _iso(log.started_at),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 "ended_at": _iso(log.ended_at),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 "next_run_at": next_runs.get(sync_type),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +                 "error": log.error_message if status == "failed" else None,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +             }
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         )
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     last_sync_at = max(last_sync_candidates) if last_sync_candidates else None
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     return {
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         "ts": _iso(datetime.now(UTC)),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         "last_sync_at": _iso(last_sync_at),
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         "running": running,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +         "jobs": jobs,
[06/08/2026, 23:06:43] +     }

[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +

[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + # Server-Sent Events: real-time sync status stream (bd ct-7d6).
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + # SSE (not WebSocket) is intentional — one-way push, EventSource auto-reconnect,
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + # works under `connect-src 'self'` CSP + cookie auth with no Azure App Service
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + # WebSocket toggle. The frontend client lives in static/js/realtimeSync.js.
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + _SSE_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 8.0
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + _SSE_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS = 3600.0  # recycle long-lived streams hourly
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + async def _sync_event_stream(request: Request):
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +     """Yield text/event-stream frames with the current sync snapshot.
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +     Opens a fresh short-lived DB session per tick (never holds a connection
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +     open for the whole stream) and stops cleanly when the client disconnects.
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +     """
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +     elapsed = 0.0
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +     # Send an initial snapshot immediately so the UI doesn't wait one interval.
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +     while elapsed <= _SSE_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS:
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +         if await request.is_disconnected():
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +             break
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +         try:
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +             with get_db_context() as db:
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +                 snapshot = build_sync_snapshot(db)
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +             payload = json.dumps(snapshot, separators=(",", ":"))
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +             yield f"event: sync\ndata: {payload}\n\n"
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +         except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - keep the stream alive on a blip
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +             # Comment frame keeps the connection warm without emitting bad data.
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +             yield ": snapshot-error\n\n"
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +         await asyncio.sleep(_SSE_INTERVAL_SECONDS)
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +         elapsed += _SSE_INTERVAL_SECONDS
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + @router.get("/stream")
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] + async def stream_sync_status(
[06/08/2026, 23:07:17] +     request: Request,

[06/08/2026, 23:12:58]
[06/08/2026, 23:13:01] Instead of testing the infinite stream through the HTTP client (which is fragile), I'll test the async generator dir[06/08/2026, 23:13:01] ectly by driving it manually—calling `anext()` to pull the first frame, then closing it. This avoids the TestClient [06/08/2026, 23:13:01] streaming complexity entirely. I'll mock the request object with an `is_disconnected()` method that returns False, a[06/08/2026, 23:13:01] nd monkeypatch `get_db_context()` to use the test database session. This way I get the first frame immediately witho[06/08/2026, 23:13:01] ut hitting the 8-second sleep, verify the frame content, and close the generator cleanly. [06/08/2026, 23:29:10] The bigger architectural issue is that the app has two separate theming systems running in parallel: DaisyUI's `data[06/08/2026, 23:29:10] -theme` on the body (for brand-specific themes) and the `.dark` class system on the html element (from darkMode.js).[06/08/2026, 23:29:10]  When dark mode toggles via the `.dark` class, DaisyUI components like badges don't respond because they're keyed to[06/08/2026, 23:29:10]  `data-theme`, not `.dark`. That's why badge-soft renders light in dark mode. The safest fix without rebuilding the [06/08/2026, 23:29:10] entire Tailwind output is to add targeted `.dark` CSS overrides in design-utilities.css for the specific badge varia[06/08/2026, 23:29:10] nts and white tiles that qa-kitten flagged.

[06/08/2026, 23:30:29]  THINKING  ⚡  I'm seeing how the theming system works—the `data-theme` attribute on the `<html>` element points to [06/08/2026, 23:30:29] the brand name (httbrands, bishops, etc.), which DaisyUI uses to apply the correct theme, while the `.light` and `.d[06/08/2026, 23:30:29] ark` classes handle the light/dark mode toggle independently without affecting the brand selection.
[06/08/2026, 23:30:29]
[06/08/2026, 23:30:31] The issue is that when dark mode is enabled, DaisyUI components still render using the brand's light theme because `[06/08/2026, 23:30:31] data-theme` never changes—so badges, cards, and other components stay light-colored against a dark background, makin[06/08/2026, 23:30:31] g them unreadable. The fix is to update `darkMode.js` so that when toggling dark mode, it swaps `data-theme` to Dais[06/08/2026, 23:30:31] yUI's built-in `dark` theme, then restores the original brand theme when switching back to light mode. I'll store th[06/08/2026, 23:30:31] e brand name from the `data-brand` attribute and use it to restore the correct theme.
[06/08/2026, 23:30:31]
[06/08/2026, 23:30:32] I also need to handle hardcoded `bg-white` utilities in the sync-dashboard templates separately, since those won't r[06/08/2026, 23:30:32] espond to the `data-theme` change—they'll need to be replaced with theme-aware classes or have dark mode overrides a[06/08/2026, 23:30:32] pplied.
[06/08/2026, 23:30:32]

[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] and identity across all our Azure tenants."*

[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] +

[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] +
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + | Page | "This page answers…" |
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + |------|----------------------|
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + | Dashboard | "Is anything on fire right now?" |
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + | Costs | "Where is the money going and is anything spiking?" |
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + | Compliance | "Which tenants/resources are out of policy?" |
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + | Resources | "What do we actually have deployed?" |
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + | Identity | "Who has access and is MFA on?" |
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + | Sync dashboard | "Is the data fresh, and did the last sync work?" |
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] +
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + ## 3. The one concept that matters: "stale" (5 min) — `OPS_ONBOARDING.md` §4
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] +

[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + - Explain: a green/blue pulsing dot = real-time connection is live; numbers you see are
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] current.
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + - Teach the reflex: **before acting on a number, glance at freshness.**
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] +
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + ## 4. Live demo — the new productivity features (8 min)
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] +
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + These shipped in ct-6vn / ct-7d6. Demo them; they make daily use faster.
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] +
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + 1. **Global search** — press <kbd>/</kbd> (or <kbd>⌘</kbd>/<kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>K</kbd>).
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] Search a tenant or user by name; jump straight to it. *"Stop hunting through menus."*
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + 2. **Keyboard shortcuts** — press <kbd>?</kbd> to open the shortcuts overlay. Demo `g` then `d`
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] (Dashboard), `g` then `c` (Costs). *"Power users never touch the mouse."*
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + 3. **Real-time sync** — open the Sync dashboard; point out status updates and toast
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] notifications appear **without refreshing** (Server-Sent Events). When a sync finishes, a toast
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] pops.
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + 4. **Dark mode** — click the  toggle (or press <kbd>t</kbd>). Note it persists across sessions.

[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + ## Appendix — common live-session pitfalls
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] +
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + - **Auth fails for one person** → don't debug live; park them on the shared screen, fix after
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] (it's almost always a missing tenant mapping — see `OPS_ONBOARDING.md` §5).
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + - **Dashboard looks empty/stale** → trigger a sync before the session, not during.
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] + - **"Why is success blue, not green?"** → intentional: the palette is colourblind-safe (blue =
[06/08/2026, 23:57:36] good). Mention it so nobody files it as a bug.

[06/08/2026, 23:58:01]
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01]
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01]  CREATE FILE  ✨ CREATE docs/runbooks/revoke-domain-intelligence-external-creds.md
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + # Runbook: Revoke domain-intelligence external credentials at source
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + **Issue:** ct-18z (P3) - carved out of ct-mql decommission (2026-06-02)
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + **Owner / executor:** Tyler (requires Cloudflare + Cloudways portal access - cannot be
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] automated by an agent)
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + **Reference:** `docs/SECRETS_OF_RECORD.md` section 5a
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + **Estimated time:** ~5 minutes
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ---
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ## Why this exists
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + When the `kv-domainiq-prod` Key Vault was deleted during the ct-mql decommission,
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + two credentials it held were for **external services**. Deleting the Azure vault
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + does NOT revoke those - they remain valid at the provider until revoked at source:
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + | Secret | Lives at | Action required |
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + |--------|----------|-----------------|
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + | `cloudflare-api-token` | Cloudflare | Revoke the API token |
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + | `CLOUDWAYS-SSH-PASSWORD` | Cloudways | Rotate/disable the SSH password |
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + Until both are revoked, a leaked copy of either value would still grant access.
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ## If you need the values (to identify which token/account)
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + The archived copies are available - read-only, for identification only:
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + - `kv-gov-prod` -> `domainiq-archived-cloudflare-api-token`
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + - `kv-gov-prod` -> `domainiq-archived-CLOUDWAYS-SSH-PASSWORD`
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + - Or recover from soft-deleted `kv-domainiq-prod` (purge-protected until **2026-08-31**).
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ```bash
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + # Identify (do NOT paste these values anywhere else):
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + az keyvault secret show --vault-name kv-gov-prod \
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +   --name domainiq-archived-cloudflare-api-token --query value -o tsv
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + az keyvault secret show --vault-name kv-gov-prod \
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +   --name domainiq-archived-CLOUDWAYS-SSH-PASSWORD --query value -o tsv
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ```
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ## Step 1 - Revoke the Cloudflare API token
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 1. Log in to the Cloudflare dashboard.
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 2. Go to **My Profile -> API Tokens** (`https://dash.cloudflare.com/profile/api-tokens`).
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 3. Find the token matching the archived value (compare the token id / name; the
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +    raw secret is only shown at creation, so match by name or last-used metadata).
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 4. Click the row's menu -> **Delete** (or **Roll** if a replacement is still needed -
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +    for decommission, **Delete** is correct).
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 5. Confirm. The token is now invalid everywhere immediately.
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ## Step 2 - Rotate/disable the Cloudways SSH password
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 1. Log in to the Cloudways platform (`https://platform.cloudways.com`).
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 2. Identify the server/account tied to the archived SSH password.
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 3. **Server Management -> Master Credentials** (or the relevant SSH/SFTP user):
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +    - Preferred: **regenerate/change** the SSH password so the archived value is dead, OR
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +    - If the server itself is being decommissioned, **delete the server/credential**.
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + 4. Save. The old password no longer authenticates.
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ## Step 3 - Confirm and close
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + - [ ] Cloudflare token deleted (or rolled) - verified gone from the API Tokens list.
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + - [ ] Cloudways SSH password rotated/disabled (or server deleted).
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + - [ ] Optional: note the date in `docs/SECRETS_OF_RECORD.md` section 5a.
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + - [ ] `bd close ct-18z` with a one-line note ("revoked at source YYYY-MM-DD").
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + ## Post-revocation: the archived copies
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] +
[06/08/2026, 23:58:01] + The `kv-gov-prod` archived copies and the soft-deleted `kv-domainiq-prod` become

[06/09/2026, 00:25:04] But given the risk of destabilizing the 4,295 passing tests, the smarter engineering call is to document and enforce[06/09/2026, 00:25:04]  the supported test invocation instead. I can document that `pytest tests/unit tests/integration` is the supported c[06/09/2026, 00:25:04] ommand (what CI runs), while `pytest tests/` mixes in architecture and chaos tests that have these async-loop intera[06/09/2026, 00:25:04] ctions. [06/09/2026, 00:27:46] Let me create a concise doc capturing the supported invocations + the event-loop isolation boundary (satisfies ct-pm[06/09/2026, 00:27:46] 3's "documented" criterion):
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58]
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58]
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58]  CREATE FILE  ✨ CREATE docs/testing/test-invocation.md
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + # Running the test suite (supported invocations)
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + > TL;DR: use `make test-ci` (or `make test-all`). **Do NOT run a bare
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + > `pytest tests/`** — it mixes test groups in one process and produces
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + > misleading order-dependent failures. See "Why" below.
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ## Supported commands
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + | Command | What it runs | When |
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + |---------|--------------|------|
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + | `make test` | `tests/unit` | fast inner loop |
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + | `make test-ci` | `tests/unit tests/integration` + coverage | mirrors CI exactly |
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + | `make test-all` | every group, **in isolated passes** | full local verification |
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + CI runs:
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ```bash
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + uv run pytest tests/unit/ tests/integration/ -m "not visual" --cov=app --cov-fail-under=35
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ```
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + This is fully green (4295 passed at time of writing).
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ## Why not `pytest tests/`?
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + The repo has test groups with very different runtime needs:
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + - `tests/unit`, `tests/integration` — the 4000+ core tests (CI gate).
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + - `tests/architecture` — fitness functions (file size, layering, security constraints).
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + - `tests/chaos` — resilience / circuit-breaker / failure-injection tests.
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + - `tests/performance` — timing-sensitive checks.
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + - `tests/e2e`, `tests/load`, `tests/smoke`, `tests/staging` — ignored by default
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +   (`addopts` in `pyproject.toml`); they need a running server or live URLs.
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + The architecture/chaos/performance suites exercise **sync↔async bridges**
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + (`asyncio.run()`, `loop.run_until_complete()`, `ThreadPoolExecutor` wrappers)
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + and run under `pytest-asyncio` `asyncio_mode = "auto"`. When they execute in the
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + **same process** as the unit tests, the collection ordering can leave a running
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + event loop active, after which later tests that bridge sync→async fail with:
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ```
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + RuntimeError: Runner.run() cannot be called from a running event loop
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ```
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + This cascades into hundreds of order-dependent failures — none of which are real
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + product defects (every group passes cleanly in its own process). To prove it:
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ```bash
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + pytest tests/unit tests/integration -m "not visual"   # green (4295)
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + pytest tests/architecture                              # green (43)
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + pytest tests/chaos                                     # green (57)
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + pytest tests/performance                               # green (3)
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ```
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + ## Enforcement
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + `make test-all` deliberately invokes each group as a **separate `pytest`
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + process** (fresh interpreter + event loop), so "run all the tests" is green and
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + trustworthy. The bare `pytest tests/` anti-pattern is documented here and in the
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + `Makefile` `test-all` target so nobody is misled by it again.
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] +
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + A future hardening (tracked in ct-pm3) could make the sync↔async bridge helpers
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + loop-safe so a single-process full run also passes, but that touches widely-used
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + runtime code and is not worth destabilizing the green suite for. The isolated-
[06/09/2026, 00:27:58] + pass boundary is the supported contract today.
[06/09/2026, 00:28:03]
[06/09/2026, 00:28:11]  THINKING  ⚡  I need to update ct-pm3 with the root cause and resolution. The acceptance criteria can be satisfied [06/09/2026, 00:28:11] either by having the tests pass cleanly or by documenting and enforcing the isolation boundary—I've done the latter [06/09/2026, 00:28:11] with documentation and the Makefile, and `make test-all isolated` passes. Even though there's potential for future h[06/09/2026, 00:28:11] ardening to make single-process work, the criteria is already met, so I'll close this as resolved.
[06/09/2026, 00:28:11]

[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] the tenant-health-strip inline script (ct-zk7)" --body "## Summary
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] **Found during pre-prod UAT.** Running the \`browser-smoke\` Playwright gate locally against merged \`main\` flagged
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] a CSP violation on **dashboard, sync-dashboard, riverside, dmarc**: an inline script executing without a valid
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] nonce.
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59]
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] ## Root cause
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] \`app/templates/macros/ds/tenant_strip.html\` shipped a **bare \`<script>\` with no nonce** (introduced in #126).
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] Every CSP variant in \`security_headers.py\` uses strict \`'nonce-{nonce}'\` with **no \`unsafe-inline\`** for
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] scripts — so the tenant-health-strip's live-update JS was **blocked by CSP in every environment, including
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] production**. Result: tenant status dots render but never update with real health. Latent, pre-existing, and
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] invisible without a real browser — exactly what UAT is for.
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59]
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] ## Fix
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] Imported macros don't receive template context, so the macro can't reach \`request.state.csp_nonce\` itself. Thread
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] it through explicitly:
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] - \`ds_tenant_strip(tenants, nonce='')\` → \`<script nonce=\"{{ nonce }}\">\`
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] - \`base.html\` passes \`request.state.csp_nonce\` at the single call site
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59]
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] No behavior change beyond the script now being allowed to run.
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59]
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] ## Verification
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] \`\`\`
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] tests/e2e/test_browser_smoke.py:  4 failed, 6 passed   (before)

[06/09/2026, 01:31:59] \`\`\`
[06/09/2026, 01:31:59]

[06/09/2026, 01:45:39] /* Global search header slot — visible on desktop (md+), hidden on mobile where
